Implementation notes

From micron reference
micron reference › Implementation notes

Compiler-required support types, architecture number tables, formatting classification, and the freestanding exception-runtime implementation. Internal names describe implementation contracts rather than stable application interfaces.

Compiler support headers

The extensionless __special headers supply compiler-recognized vocabulary where a full standard-library header is unavailable: initializer_list, comparison categories, coroutine handles/awaiters, reflection support, and hosted pthread declarations. Their use of std and ABI namespaces is required by the corresponding compiler/runtime protocols.

__special/pthread is a set of external C declarations and architecture-sized opaque records. It avoids including the full system pthread header, but does not implement pthread_create/join or the hosted runtime. The hosted backend still needs compatible externally linked symbols. The freestanding thread backend supplies its own clone/TLS protocol elsewhere. __special/index_sequence is currently empty; the implemented micron index-sequence traits reside with the type utilities.

Architecture and container plumbing

The four __syscall_codes_* headers supply compile-time number tables and selected SYS_* aliases. A base template parameter shifts a table's numbers; ARM additionally supplies EABI and OABI numeric aliases. This data neither changes the assembly calling convention nor probes kernel support. Select the correct target through syscall.hpp rather than mixing architecture tables in one translation unit.

bits/__abc_mt.hpp resolves the freestanding allocator's multithreading gate before allocator configuration; including it too late reports a compile error. The backoff and pause helpers select target operations without allocating. The map visitor chooses a for_each interface or a supported key/value iterator representation; map rebuild selects persistent value replacement or mutable insertion. Those helpers inherit their selected container's allocation, ordering, and complexity.

bits/__print.hpp classifies semantic renderers, node chains, maps/sets, sum/product values, dense/sparse shapes, sequences, pointer wrappers, and opaque tagged objects at compile time. It emits collection/product/vector delimiters and recursively formats elements. Hash/heap traversal follows storage order, while opaque concurrent containers render a summary. A consuming range is rejected instead of silently traversed. Rendering cost follows visited elements and nested formatting; this classification header supplies no I/O sink.

Exception runtime selection

__micron_eh enables the library's compiler ABI entry points. Native amd64/i386/AArch64 use DWARF .eh_frame metadata and a two-phase search/cleanup walk; ARM32 uses .ARM.exidx/.ARM.extab compact unwinding with C++ catch metadata. WebAssembly selects its separate compiler exception backend. Without the gate, these native runtime definitions are absent.

Caught/uncaught exception bookkeeping is thread-local by default. MICRON_NO_TLS selects process-wide single-threaded exception storage; it does not add synchronization for concurrent throws. Native exception allocation uses an independent raw mmap path with a prefix and ABI header, then falls back to 16 atomic-claimed 1024-byte emergency slots when a complete allocation fits. Exhaustion terminates. It does not route exception allocation through a container allocator.

The native termination handler writes a diagnostic and calls micron::abort(6), whose native implementation exits rather than delivering SIGABRT. Custom handler slots are ordinary unsynchronized globals. Unhandled exceptions and malformed/unavailable unwind paths are not recoverable status-returning operations.

Unwinding implementation limits

DWARF discovery reads the running executable's __ehdr_start/program headers and its PT_GNU_EH_FRAME table, then binary-searches fixed-width FDE entries. It does not maintain a general registry of arbitrary loaded modules' unwind tables. DWARF64-length records and variable-width search tables are rejected. CFI interpretation uses fixed register arrays, a 64-word expression stack, and eight remembered rows; it implements a subset of DWARF operations and expects trusted compiler-emitted metadata.

The native register model restores core registers; AArch64 FP/SIMD register restoration is absent. ARM compact unwinding similarly advances past saved VFP/iWMMXt storage without restoring its register values. ARM _Unwind_ForcedUnwind currently delegates to ordinary exception raising and ignores the stop callback. These paths are narrower than a complete platform unwinder/cancellation implementation.

FDE/exidx search is O(log F) for F indexed functions. Each frame then interprets its CFI/opcode program and scans relevant catch actions, so throw cost depends on frames and metadata length. There is no fixed constant-time throw guarantee. The RTTI support handles identity and limited base/pointer matching; its multiple/virtual-inheritance traversal does not apply recorded base offsets or disambiguation and is not a complete general dynamic-cast implementation.

std::initializer_list

Defined in header <micron/__special/initializer_list>.

Compiler-created non-owning view of a const element array, represented by a pointer and count.

Lifetime and access

The default list is empty. begin/end and size inspect the compiler-provided array; copying the list copies its view and does not copy or own its elements. Retaining the list does not independently extend the backing array's lifetime beyond the language's initializer-list lifetime rules. Iteration is read-only and linear in elements, with constant-time construction/copy/access and no allocation by the wrapper.

std::coroutine_handle

Defined in header <micron/__special/coroutine>.

Non-owning compiler coroutine-frame address with resume/destroy/done operations and optional typed promise access.

Frame protocol

The void specialization erases the promise type; a typed handle additionally obtains a frame from a promise and recovers that promise with compiler builtins. from_address does not allocate or validate a frame. Copying/destroying the handle value has no frame-lifetime effect. destroy explicitly destroys the frame, while resume/done require a valid frame in the appropriate suspended state; checking the handle's boolean only tests non-null.

coroutine_traits extracts Result::promise_type when present. suspend_always always suspends, and suspend_never is immediately ready. noop_coroutine returns a permanent special handle whose operations do nothing, whose boolean is true, and whose done is false. The supplied hash specialization hashes the numeric frame address. These primitives do not schedule tasks or synchronize access to a frame.

pthread_attr_t

Defined in header <micron/__special/pthread>.

Architecture-sized opaque hosted pthread attribute record declared for the C ABI.

Opaque record family

pthread_t identifies a hosted thread; pthread_once_t is an int and pthread_spinlock_t a volatile int. Attribute, mutex/mutex-attribute, condition/condition-attribute, rwlock/rwlock-attribute, and barrier/barrier-attribute unions reserve architecture-selected byte sizes and required alignment. They contain no C++ ownership or automatic initialization/destruction.

Use the corresponding external initialization/operation/destruction calls and a compatible hosted implementation. Copying opaque bytes is not a synchronization or resource-transfer contract. pthread_key_t and the key create/delete/get/set declarations support host-managed thread-specific values and destructors; they do not supply a local key table in this header.

pthread_key_t

Defined in header <micron/__special/pthread_key>.

Unsigned hosted thread-specific-storage key passed to external pthread key operations.

Ownership

The key is a numeric identifier. Its value neither owns the per-thread pointer nor invokes its destructor automatically as a C++ object. Key creation associates an optional host destructor callback, and deleting the key uses the external pthread protocol. These declarations require the selected host runtime to provide their definitions.

micron::cpuid_regs

Defined in header <micron/bits/__cpuid.hpp>.

Four u32 words holding EAX, EBX, ECX, and EDX from an x86 CPUID query.

Availability

The value record is declared on all targets, while the __cpuid_read/max-leaf helpers are emitted only for x86. i386 PIC preserves the GOT register around CPUID. This support header offers explicit feature queries; its presence does not make ordinary SIMD wrappers automatically dispatch at runtime.

amd64_syscalls

Defined in header <micron/bits/__syscall_codes_amd64.hpp>.

Compile-time amd64 syscall-number table with a base-adjusted nested nr namespace-like structure.

Number table

amd64_native selects base zero and the SYS_* constants alias its entries. These are signed long constants for the x86-64 Linux ABI. The template stores no runtime state and performs no kernel call. The number table includes historical and newer entries independently of the running kernel.

i386_syscalls

Defined in header <micron/bits/__syscall_codes_i386.hpp>.

Compile-time i386 syscall-number table with native, legacy, and time64 entry numbers.

Number table

i386_native selects base zero and SYS_* aliases use its nested nr constants, typed as int. Legacy setuid/getuid numbers differ from the explicit 32-bit-UID forms, and legacy mmap differs from mmap2. The typed wrappers select the appropriate call and record layout; the number alone does not perform conversion.

arm32_syscalls

Defined in header <micron/bits/__syscall_codes_arm32.hpp>.

Compile-time ARM32 number table with EABI/OABI base aliases and ARM-private SWI values.

Number table

arm32_eabi uses base zero and supplies the active SYS_* aliases; arm32_oabi uses base 0x900000. The template adjusts numeric constants only. Selecting the OABI alias does not replace the svc EABI calling sequence in the native syscall wrapper. Private cache/TLS/breakpoint numbers occupy the ARM-private range.

arm64_syscalls

Defined in header <micron/bits/__syscall_codes_arm64.hpp>.

Compile-time AArch64 Linux syscall-number table and zero-base native alias.

Number table

arm64_native exposes signed-long constants through nr and SYS_* aliases. The asm-generic ABI omits several older calls, such as poll, pause, and epoll_wait; higher wrappers use available ppoll/epoll_pwait forms. Table entries are constant data and do not establish runtime feature availability.

std::type_info

Defined in header <micron/bits/eh/typeinfo.hpp>.

Freestanding compiler RTTI base with name comparison and virtual catch/upcast hooks under __micron_eh.

ABI hierarchy

The record contains its compiler ABI vptr and name pointer. name removes a leading internal '*' marker; equality compares pointer identity or eligible name text, and before orders the names/pointers. Copying is disabled. Compiler-emitted derived records represent fundamental/array/function/enum/class, single-base/multiple-base, pointer, and pointer-to-member types.

The derived layouts and virtual member order follow the compiler ABI. Their implementations supply identity, single-base recursion, and limited pointer qualification/catch matching; multiple/virtual base offsets are not applied. Do not construct these records as a portable replacement for complete hosted RTTI or assume the declared hierarchy implements general dynamic_cast behavior.

_Unwind_Exception

Defined in header <micron/bits/eh/unwind.hpp>.

Compiler exception-control header used by the selected native unwinder ABI.

Backend-specific representation

DWARF targets store exception class, cleanup callback, and private unwinder words in the aligned control header. ARM EHABI instead stores class bytes plus unwinder/barrier/cleanup/personality caches. _Unwind_Context is an opaque declaration here and has backend-specific register/frame metadata definitions.

Reason/action enums and callback aliases describe the compiler unwinding protocol. Application code normally throws/catches through language syntax and does not manufacture these control records. Cleanup callback and exception-object lifetimes belong to that protocol; copying its bytes does not duplicate a live exception.

Functions

Each entry describes one overload. Select an entry for its declaration, effects, return expressions, and source evidence.
